Faults: Arching the low back; Shrugging; Forcing range
Cautions: Very shoulder friendly.
Progress by: Add a band or lift off at the top
Regress by: Smaller range, floor angels
Default prescription: 1 to 2 sets x 5 to 10 slow reps (or 30 to 60s), in warm up or cool down | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID, BURN
Sources: Cressey, DeFranco (Simple 6), Squat University

FAMILY serratus | Shoulder: serratus anterior
Muscles: Serratus anterior
Why: The serratus holds the shoulder blade against the ribcage and rotates it upward for overhead reaching. Weak serratus = winging and shoulder pain.
Cues: 1) Arms straight 2) Push the floor or weight away, spreading the shoulder blades 3) Return slowly
Faults: Bending the elbows; Shrugging; Sagging hips (push up plus)
Cautions: Very shoulder friendly.
Progress by: Push up plus, band punch, heavier
Regress by: Wall version
Default prescription: 2 to 3 sets x 10 to 15 reps, tempo 2-1-1-1, rest 45 to 60s, RPE 8 | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID
Sources: Cressey, Squat University

FAMILY pistol | Single leg squat (advanced)
Muscles: Quads, glutes, adductors, balance and foot control
Why: Single leg squats are the top of Mike Boyle's single leg ladder: maximum leg strength with minimal spinal load, and they expose left/right imbalances. Box versions control depth so they are accessible far earlier than a full pistol.
Cues: 1) Stand on one leg, other leg held forward (pistol) or behind (skater/shrimp) 2) Sit back and down slowly to the box or depth target, knee over the middle of the foot 3) Drive up through the whole foot without the knee caving
Faults: Knee diving inward; Collapsing onto the box; Heel lifting
Cautions: Demanding on the knee. Use a box and TRX support first. Not for acute knee pain or early post surgery.
Progress by: Lower the box, remove support, add a light counterweight held forward, then load
Regress by: Higher box, TRX assisted, step downs, split squat
Default prescription: 3 sets x 5 to 8 each leg, slow lowering | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID
Sources: Boyle, Squat University, Gambetta

FAMILY step_down | Single leg: eccentric step down
Muscles: Quads, glutes, hip stabilisers
Why: Controlled lowering from a step builds knee control and eccentric quad strength, the exact quality needed for walking downstairs. Also a key screening test for knee valgus and hip control.
Cues: 1) Stand on a box, other leg hanging off the front or side 2) Lower slowly by bending the standing knee, hips back slightly 3) Tap the heel lightly and return without pushing off the floor
Faults: Knee diving in; Hip dropping on the free side; Crashing down to the floor
Cautions: Excellent for knee rehab when the box is low. Raise the height as control improves.
Progress by: Higher box, hold weight, slower lowering
Regress by: Lower box, hand support, Poliquin step up
Default prescription: 2 to 3 sets x 8 to 12 each leg, 3s lowering | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID
Sources: Squat University, Poliquin, Boyle

FAMILY walking_lunge | Single leg: forward / walking lunge
Muscles: Quads, glutes, hamstrings, balance, grip when loaded
Why: Forward and walking lunges add deceleration and balance: you have to control the body moving forward over the front leg. Very functional and very metabolic, so they suit BURN and HYBRID sessions. Loaded carry lunges add grip and core.
Cues: 1) Long step forward, land heel first 2) Drop the back knee down, front knee over the middle of the foot 3) Push through the front foot and step through to the next rep
Faults: Short choppy steps that load the front knee; Front knee caving; Torso collapsing forward
Cautions: More knee stress than reverse lunges. Swap to reverse lunges or split squats with knee pain. Needs space in groups.
Progress by: Add load (DB, KB rack, overhead), pulses, longer distance
Regress by: Reverse lunge, split squat, hand support
Default prescription: 3 sets x 8 to 12 reps, tempo 3-0-1-1, rest 60 to 90s, RPE 7 to 9 (each leg) or 20 to 30m | Sessions: BURN, HYBRID, STRONG
Sources: Gambetta (lunge matrix), Cosgrove, Paul Chek (lunge pattern)

FAMILY poliquin_step | Single leg: knee over toes step (VMO)
Muscles: Quads (VMO emphasis), knee tendons, ankle
Why: Low step variations where the knee deliberately travels forward over the toes with the heel raised (Poliquin step up) or from the toes (Patrick step). They load the inner quad and patellar tendon in a controlled way, which is why Poliquin, Ben Patrick and physios use them for knee pain and knee resilience. Small range, huge payoff.
Cues: 1) Stand on a low step, working heel raised on a small plate or wedge (Poliquin) or on the toes (Patrick) 2) Slowly lower the other heel towards the floor by bending the working knee forward over the toes 3) Lightly tap and drive back up through the front of the foot, keeping the knee in line
Faults: Hips shifting back instead of the knee travelling forward; Knee caving inward; Dropping fast instead of controlling the lowering
Cautions: Knee friendly when kept pain free. Start with a very low step (5 to 10cm) and hand support. Ideal in every knee pain programme.
Progress by: Higher step, slower tempo, hold DBs, progress to step downs and front foot elevated split squats
Regress by: Lower step, hand on the rig, partial range
Default prescription: 2 to 3 sets x 10 to 20 each leg, slow lowering (3s) | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID
Sources: Poliquin, Ben Patrick (ATG), Squat University

FAMILY lateral_lunge | Single leg: lateral (frontal plane)
Muscles: Adductors, glutes, quads, hip mobility
Why: Most training is forwards and backwards; lateral lunges train the side to side plane, building adductor strength and hip mobility that protect against groin strains and help golf, tennis and walking on uneven ground. Cossack squats are a great mobility and strength hybrid (Squat University and DeFranco's Limber 11).
Cues: 1) Step wide to the side, toes forward or slightly out 2) Sit back into one hip, keeping the other leg straight 3) Push back to the start through the bent leg
Faults: Knee caving on the bent leg; Rounding the back to get lower; Heel lifting
Cautions: Groin and hip sensitive members: reduce range. Assisted versions (holding the rig) for seniors.
Progress by: Add load (goblet, DB, landmine), slider, deeper cossack
Regress by: Shorter range, hand support, lateral lunge rock (mobility)
Default prescription: 3 sets x 8 to 12 reps, tempo 3-0-1-1, rest 60 to 90s, RPE 7 to 9 (each side) | Sessions: HYBRID, STRONG, BURN
Sources: Gambetta (multi plane), DeFranco (Limber 11), Squat University

FAMILY curtsy | Single leg: lunge (rotational/adductor bias)
Muscles: Glutes (especially glute med), adductors, quads
Why: Stepping back and across challenges the hips in a different plane, hitting the side glutes and inner thighs. Good variety and good for hip stability and golf.
Cues: 1) Step one foot back and across behind the other 2) Lower straight down with the front knee tracking the toes 3) Push through the front foot to return
Faults: Twisting the hips excessively; Front knee caving; Losing balance from too narrow a step
Cautions: Can irritate knees and hips if the range is forced. Keep it pain free.
Progress by: Add load, deficit, pair with a single leg RDL
Regress by: Shorter range, hand support, reverse lunge
Default prescription: 3 sets x 8 to 12 reps, tempo 3-0-1-1, rest 60 to 90s, RPE 7 to 9 (each side) | Sessions: HYBRID, BURN
Sources: Gambetta (multi plane), Cosgrove

FAMILY reverse_lunge | Single leg: reverse lunge
Muscles: Quads, glutes, hamstrings
Why: Stepping backwards keeps the shin more vertical, so it is far kinder to the knees than forward lunges while still training single leg strength and balance. The default lunge at Optimal. Deficit and slider versions increase range and control.
Cues: 1) Stand tall, step one foot back 2) Drop the back knee towards the floor, front shin fairly vertical 3) Drive through the front heel to stand back up
Faults: Stepping too short; Front knee collapsing; Leaning forward and rounding
Cautions: Most knee friendly lunge. Use a pad and hand support for beginners.
Progress by: Add load, deficit (front foot on a plate), knee drive at the top, barbell/landmine
Regress by: Split squat (static), hand support, shorter range
Default prescription: 3 to 4 sets x 6 to 10 reps, tempo 3-1-1-0, rest 90 to 120s, RPE 7 to 8 (each leg) | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID, BURN
Sources: Boyle, Cosgrove, Squat University

FAMILY split_squat | Single leg: split squat
Muscles: Quads, glutes, adductors
Why: The entry point of the single leg ladder. A static split stance gives a stable base, so people learn to load one leg at a time safely. It builds balance, fixes imbalances and is far easier on the back than heavy bilateral squats. Front foot elevated adds knee travel (ATG style); cable and landmine versions add stability.
Cues: 1) Long split stance, both feet pointing forward, hip width apart 2) Lower the back knee straight down towards the floor 3) Push through the front foot to stand, keep the torso tall
Faults: Stance too narrow (tightrope), losing balance; Front heel lifting; Lunging forward instead of dropping down
Cautions: Knee friendly when depth is controlled. Use a pad under the back knee. Hold a support for balance in seniors.
Progress by: Add load, elevate the front foot (ATG style), then elevate the back foot (RFESS)
Regress by: TRX or rig assisted split squat, shorter range, isometric split squat hold
Default prescription: 3 to 4 sets x 6 to 10 reps, tempo 3-1-1-0, rest 90 to 120s, RPE 7 to 8 (each leg) | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID
Sources: Boyle, Squat University, Ben Patrick (front foot elevated)

FAMILY rfess | Single leg: split squat (rear foot elevated)
Muscles: Quads, glutes, adductors, hip flexor stretch on the back leg
Why: Mike Boyle's signature lower body lift. Huge leg strength with far less spinal load than heavy bilateral squats, fixes left/right differences, and stretches the back leg's hip flexor (great for desk workers). Lean forward slightly for more glutes, stay upright for more quads.
Cues: 1) Back foot laces down on a bench, front foot far enough forward that the front heel stays down 2) Drop the back knee straight down, front knee tracks over the toes 3) Drive through the front foot, keep the hips square
Faults: Front foot too close so the heel lifts; Pushing off the back foot; Hips twisting or knee caving
Cautions: Knee friendly when the depth is controlled. A lower bench helps tight hip flexors. Balance can limit beginners: hold the rig with one hand.
Progress by: Add load (DBs, then double KB rack, landmine, SSB/barbell), pause, 1.5 reps, deficit (front foot on a plate)
Regress by: Split squat with back foot on the floor, hand support, reduce depth
Default prescription: 3 to 4 sets x 6 to 10 reps, tempo 3-1-1-0, rest 90 to 120s, RPE 7 to 8 (each leg) | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID
Sources: Boyle, DeFranco, Cosgrove

FAMILY isometric_split | Single leg: split squat isometric
Muscles: Quads, glutes, hip flexors
Why: Holding the bottom of a split squat builds strength and tendon tolerance at the exact point people are weakest, with very low joint irritation. Great for knee pain, beginners and as a pre-lift primer.
Cues: 1) Get into a split squat with both knees around 90 degrees 2) Back knee hovering just off the floor 3) Hold, breathe, push the front foot into the floor
Faults: Leaning on the front thigh; Front knee caving; Holding the breath
Cautions: Very knee friendly. Raise the back knee position if painful.
Progress by: Hold longer, hold DBs, progress to dynamic split squats
Regress by: Higher position, hand support
Default prescription: 3 to 5 x 20 to 45s holds, rest 60s, about 70% effort each leg | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID
Sources: Rehab research (isometrics), Squat University

FAMILY step_up | Single leg: step up
Muscles: Quads, glutes, hamstrings, balance
Why: A real life movement (stairs, kerbs, climbing). Very back friendly, easy to scale by box height, and the concentric emphasis means little soreness. Knee drive and crossover versions add balance and hip work.
Cues: 1) Whole foot on the box, knee over the middle of the foot 2) Lean forward slightly and drive through the box leg to stand tall 3) Step down with control, don't push off the bottom leg
Faults: Pushing off the floor leg; Knee caving as you stand; Box too high so the hip hikes
Cautions: Knee friendly at lower heights. Use a low box and hand support for seniors and knee pain. Faster 'step ups' in BURN keep impact low.
Progress by: Higher box, add DBs/goblet/barbell, add a knee drive, slower lowering
Regress by: Lower box, hand support, bodyweight
Default prescription: 3 to 4 sets x 6 to 10 reps, tempo 3-1-1-0, rest 90 to 120s, RPE 7 to 8 (each leg) | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID, BURN
Sources: Boyle, Cosgrove, Gambetta

FAMILY soft_tissue | Soft tissue / mobilisation
Muscles: Target tissues
Why: Foam rolling reduces the sensation of stiffness and prepares tissues for movement (Cosgrove template step 1). Short, targeted, before the session.
Cues: 1) Roll slowly 2) Pause on tender spots and breathe 3) 30 to 60s per area
Faults: Rolling on the low back; Too long; Rolling acute injuries
Cautions: Avoid rolling directly on bones, varicose veins or acute injuries.
Progress by: Firmer tools
Regress by: Softer roller
Default prescription: 30 to 60s per area | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID, BURN
Sources: Cosgrove, DeFranco (Limber 11)

FAMILY thruster | Squat + vertical push (combination)
Muscles: Quads, glutes, shoulders, triceps, core
Why: Links a squat and an overhead press into one total body movement. Very metabolic, great for BURN and HYBRID sessions, and the legs help drive the weight overhead so people can press more.
Cues: 1) Squat with the weight in the rack 2) Drive up hard and use that momentum to press overhead 3) Lock out with biceps by the ears, ribs down, then lower back to the rack
Faults: Pressing before the legs finish the drive; Arching the low back at the top; Losing the rack position and squatting with the elbows dropped
Cautions: Needs pain free overhead range. With shoulder pain swap to squat to landmine press. Watch technique as fatigue builds in circuits.
Progress by: Add load, double KBs, barbell, more rounds or density
Regress by: Goblet squat to press, landmine squat to press, split into separate squat and press
Default prescription: 3 to 4 sets x 8 to 12 reps, or 30 to 40s in circuits | Sessions: BURN, HYBRID
Sources: Cosgrove (MRT), DeFranco

FAMILY trx_squat | Squat: assisted
Muscles: Quads, glutes
Why: Holding the TRX lets people counterbalance, so they can sit deeper and more upright than they could alone. It is the go to G1 squat for beginners, seniors, heavy members and anyone with knee or hip pain, and it builds confidence fast.
Cues: 1) Hold the handles at chest height with arms straight 2) Sit back and down, letting the straps take a little weight 3) Use the legs to stand, the arms only steady you
Faults: Pulling with the arms to stand up; Leaning back so far the feet slide; Squatting deeper than the pain free range
Cautions: Very joint friendly. Ideal first squat after injury or replacement surgery (within surgeon's limits).
Progress by: Use the straps less, slower tempo, add a pause, progress to goblet box squat
Regress by: Squat to a high box with the TRX, reduce depth
Default prescription: 3 sets x 10 to 15 reps, tempo 3-1-1-0 | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID, BURN
Sources: Cosgrove (regress to progress), Boyle, Squat University

FAMILY overhead_squat | Squat: bilateral overhead (mobility/stability test)
Muscles: Quads, glutes, upper back, shoulders, core
Why: The overhead squat exposes stiffness at the ankles, hips, T spine and shoulders all at once, which is why Squat University and the FMS use it as a screen. With a dowel it is an excellent mobility drill; loaded it is a high skill G4 lift.
Cues: 1) Arms locked overhead, biceps by the ears, push up into the dowel or weight 2) Sit down between the hips with the chest proud 3) Keep the bar over the middle of the foot throughout
Faults: Arms drifting forward; Lower back rounding at the bottom; Heels lifting
Cautions: Shoulder demanding: avoid loading with shoulder pain or impingement. Use a dowel as a warm up screen instead.
Progress by: Dowel → light DB/plate → barbell overhead squat
Regress by: Dowel only, squat to a box, heels elevated, arms in a Y position
Default prescription: 2 to 3 sets x 5 to 8 reps, light, slow | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID
Sources: Squat University, Gray Cook (FMS), Poliquin

FAMILY suitcase_squat | Squat: bilateral with offset load
Muscles: Quads, glutes, obliques (anti lateral flexion)
Why: Weights at the sides lower the centre of mass and make it a hybrid of squat and deadlift. Single side loading adds an anti lateral flexion core challenge. Easy to teach and very back friendly.
Cues: 1) Weights by the sides, arms long 2) Sit down between the heels keeping the chest tall 3) Stand up without leaning to the loaded side
Faults: Rounding the back to reach lower; Leaning toward the weight; Shrugging the shoulders
Cautions: Back friendly. Grip may limit load.
Progress by: Heavier, single side only, tempo
Regress by: Lighter weights, squat to a box
Default prescription: 3 sets x 8 to 12 reps, tempo 3-0-1-1, rest 60 to 90s, RPE 7 to 9 | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID, BURN
Sources: Dan John, Cosgrove

FAMILY sit_to_stand | Squat: functional (sit to stand)
Muscles: Quads, glutes, balance
Why: The most important real life squat: getting up from a chair or toilet. A key longevity skill and a core test for seniors (30 second sit to stand). Done fast on the way up, it trains power, which declines faster than strength with age.
Cues: 1) Sit tall on a box or chair, feet slightly back under the knees 2) Lean the chest forward, push the floor away and stand up tall 3) Sit back down slowly with control, don't flop
Faults: Using the hands to push off; Feet too far forward; Collapsing back down onto the seat
Cautions: Suitable for almost everyone. Raise the seat height for painful knees or hips. Single leg version needs good knee control.
Progress by: Lower the box, hold a weight at the chest, stand up as fast as possible (power), single leg sit to stand
Regress by: Raise the seat, use hands on thighs or a TRX for help, reduce reps
Default prescription: 3 sets x 8 to 12 reps (or max reps in 30s for testing), stand up fast, lower slowly | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID
Sources: Seniors research (functional testing), Gambetta (fundamental movement), Attia (centenarian decathlon)

FAMILY wall_sit | Squat: isometric
Muscles: Quads, glutes, adductors
Why: A simple, joint friendly way to build quad strength and endurance with zero technique demand. Isometrics can reduce knee pain and are ideal for beginners, seniors and anyone who cannot yet squat through full range. The Swiss ball version lets people practise the squat path with support.
Cues: 1) Back flat against the wall, feet hip width and slightly in front 2) Slide down until the knees are at a pain free angle (aim 90 degrees over time) 3) Weight through the whole foot, breathe slowly, knees in line with toes
Faults: Hands pushing on the thighs; Knees caving in; Going too deep too soon and aggravating knees
Cautions: Very knee friendly at higher positions. Adjust depth to stay at or below 3/10 pain. Avoid long holds with breath holding for members with high blood pressure.
Progress by: Lower the position, hold longer, hold a plate, progress to single leg wall sit or tempo goblet squats
Regress by: Higher position, shorter holds, hands on thighs for the first few sessions
Default prescription: 3 to 5 x 20 to 45s holds, rest 60s, about 70% effort | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID, BURN
Sources: Squat University, Rehab research (isometrics), Ben Patrick

FAMILY sissy | Squat: knee dominant (quad lengthening)
Muscles: Quads (rectus femoris in a lengthened position), hip flexors
Why: Trains the quads with the knees travelling far over the toes and the hips extended. Builds strength in the end range of knee bending, which Ben Patrick uses to bulletproof knees. Assisted versions are surprisingly accessible.
Cues: 1) Hold a post or TRX for support 2) Rise onto the toes, push the knees forward and lean the body back as one straight line from knee to shoulder 3) Lower only as far as pain free, drive back up through the quads
Faults: Bending at the hips (turns it into a normal squat); Dropping too deep too early; Letting go of the support before ready
Cautions: Earn it. Start short range with hand support. Avoid with acute knee pain, recent knee surgery or knee replacements unless cleared.
Progress by: Increase range, reduce hand assistance, add a plate at the chest, progress towards reverse Nordic
Regress by: Short range only, more hand support, swap to Spanish squat or heels elevated goblet squat
Default prescription: 2 to 3 sets x 6 to 12 reps, slow lowering, hand support | Sessions: STRONG
Sources: Ben Patrick (ATG), Poliquin (VMO work)

FAMILY bodyweight_squat | Squat: knee dominant bilateral
Muscles: Quads, glutes, adductors
Why: The foundation squat pattern. Builds the movement before load and is useful for warm ups, high rep BURN work and beginners. Band and pause variations add a twist: mini bands cue the knees out, pulses keep tension, dead stops kill the bounce.
Cues: 1) Feet shoulder width, toes slightly out, tripod foot 2) Sit down between the hips, knees track over the toes 3) Stand tall and squeeze the glutes
Faults: Heels lifting; Knees caving; Folding forward at the hips
Cautions: Generally joint friendly. Use a box with knee pain, heel elevation with stiff ankles.
Progress by: Add a pause, tempo, then load (goblet squat)
Regress by: Squat to a box, TRX assisted squat
Default prescription: 2 to 3 sets x 12 to 20 reps (or 30 to 40s) | Sessions: BURN, HYBRID
Sources: Squat University, Paul Chek (Primal Pattern), Dan John

FAMILY landmine_squat | Squat: knee dominant bilateral
Muscles: Quads, glutes, core
Why: The landmine's arc lets people lean into the bar and squat with an upright torso and a guided path. It is very back and shoulder friendly and a great bridge between goblet and barbell squatting.
Cues: 1) Hold the end of the bar at the chest, lean slightly into it 2) Feet slightly in front of the body, sit down and back 3) Drive up into the bar, finish tall
Faults: Feet too close to the bar so the knees jam; Rounding the back; Pushing the bar away with the arms
Cautions: Shoulder friendly (no bar on the back). Hack squat version loads knees more: control depth with knee pain.
Progress by: Add plates, add a press at the top, pause reps
Regress by: Lighter load, reduce depth, goblet box squat
Default prescription: 3 to 4 sets x 6 to 10 reps, tempo 3-1-1-0, rest 90 to 120s, RPE 7 to 8 | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID
Sources: DeFranco, Cosgrove

FAMILY goblet | Squat: knee dominant bilateral
Muscles: Quads, glutes, adductors, upper back, core
Why: Holding the weight at the chest counterbalances the body, so people naturally squat more upright and deeper with better form. Dan John and Squat University both use it as THE teaching squat, and it is strong enough to stay useful for years. Fits groups perfectly because load scales from a 4kg KB to a 40kg+ DB.
Cues: 1) Hold the bell tight to the chest, elbows pointing down 2) Push the knees out and sit between the hips, chest proud 3) Drive up through the whole foot, squeeze the glutes at the top
Faults: Rounding the upper back and letting the weight pull you forward; Heels lifting; Knees caving inward on the way up
Cautions: Knee and back friendly. Heels elevated version helps stiff ankles. Use a box for depth control with knee pain. Upper back can fatigue first as loads get heavy.
Progress by: Add load, tempo (4-1-1-0), pause at the bottom, 1.5 reps, move to double KB front squat or SSB squat
Regress by: Goblet squat to a box, TRX assisted squat, lighter bell
Default prescription: 3 to 4 sets x 6 to 10 reps, tempo 3-1-1-0, rest 90 to 120s, RPE 7 to 8 | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID, BURN
Sources: Dan John, Squat University, Cosgrove, Boyle

FAMILY back_squat | Squat: knee dominant bilateral (barbell)
Muscles: Quads, glutes, adductors, spinal erectors
Why: The classic heavy squat. Builds strength, muscle and bone density (LIFTMOR used heavy squats). Pin and Anderson squats start from the bottom, removing the bounce and building strength out of the hole. At Optimal it is a G4 lift for members with good mobility and no back or shoulder restriction; the SSB is the default alternative.
Cues: 1) Bar on the upper back, hands tight, elbows under the bar 2) Big breath and brace, sit between the hips with knees tracking the toes 3) Drive up keeping the chest and hips rising together
Faults: Hips shooting up first (good morning squat); Losing the brace and rounding at the bottom; Knees caving under load
Cautions: High spinal load. Not for active back pain. Needs shoulder external rotation to hold the bar: use SSB or trap bar squat if shoulders are cranky.
Progress by: Add load, pause reps, tempo, 1.25 reps, rep maxes (Teams G4 only)
Regress by: SSB squat, box squat, goblet or double KB front squat
Default prescription: 3 to 4 sets x 6 to 10 reps, tempo 3-1-1-0, rest 90 to 120s, RPE 7 to 8 | Sessions: STRONG
Sources: DeFranco, Squat University, LIFTMOR research, Poliquin

FAMILY box_squat | Squat: knee dominant bilateral (depth controlled)
Muscles: Quads, glutes, adductors, hamstrings
Why: The box sets a consistent, safe depth and teaches people to sit back and control the descent. It is the best teaching squat for beginners and painful knees and, with a barbell or SSB, a classic DeFranco/Westside strength builder.
Cues: 1) Box at a height where the spine stays neutral 2) Sit back and down with control, touch the box lightly (don't crash) 3) Stay braced, drive up through the whole foot
Faults: Plonking down and relaxing on the box; Rocking back to get up; Knees caving as they stand
Cautions: Excellent for knee pain and older members: adjust height to the pain free range. Lower the box as mobility and control improve.
Progress by: Lower the box, add load (goblet then SSB/barbell), pause on the box without relaxing, remove the box
Regress by: Raise the box, TRX assisted box squat, sit to stand
Default prescription: 3 to 4 sets x 6 to 10 reps, tempo 3-1-1-0, rest 90 to 120s, RPE 7 to 8 | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID
Sources: DeFranco (Westside), Squat University, Cosgrove

FAMILY front_rack_squat | Squat: knee dominant bilateral (front loaded)
Muscles: Quads, glutes, upper back, core
Why: Front loading keeps the torso upright, puts more work on the quads and core, and puts less shear on the low back than back squatting. The double KB front squat is a great G3 group squat; the barbell front squat is a G4 strength lift for those with the wrist and thoracic mobility.
Cues: 1) Bells or bar in a tight rack, elbows high and forward 2) Brace, sit straight down between the hips 3) Keep the elbows up as you drive up
Faults: Elbows dropping and the chest falling forward; Wrists bent back painfully (barbell); Losing tension at the bottom
Cautions: Low back friendlier than back squats. Barbell front rack needs wrist, lat and T spine mobility. Zercher loads the elbows: use padding.
Progress by: Add load, pause at the bottom, tempo, progress DB → double KB → barbell front squat
Regress by: Goblet squat, box to control depth, lighter load
Default prescription: 3 to 4 sets x 6 to 10 reps, tempo 3-1-1-0, rest 90 to 120s, RPE 7 to 8 | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID
Sources: Squat University, Boyle, Dan John

FAMILY ssb_squat | Squat: knee dominant bilateral (loaded)
Muscles: Quads, glutes, adductors, upper back
Why: The safety squat bar lets members back squat heavy without needing shoulder mobility to hold a straight bar, and its forward pitch keeps the torso upright. It is Optimal's main heavy squat tool for 40+ members with cranky shoulders, elbows or wrists. DeFranco built his reputation using it.
Cues: 1) Handles in front, pads on the upper back, brace 360 degrees before descending 2) Push knees out over the middle of the foot, sit between the hips 3) Drive up with the chest leading, don't let the bar tip you forward
Faults: Letting the bar pull the chest forward and rounding the upper back; Losing the brace at the bottom; Cutting depth as the weight goes up
Cautions: Shoulder and wrist friendly. Still a spinal load: check back history. For knee pain use a box to control depth.
Progress by: Add load (double progression), pause squats, tempo 4-1-X-0, SSB split squats, rep maxes on rotating variations (Teams G4)
Regress by: SSB box squat to a higher box, goblet squat, front loaded DB squat
Default prescription: 3 to 4 sets x 6 to 10 reps, tempo 3-1-1-0, rest 90 to 120s, RPE 7 to 8 | Sessions: STRONG
Sources: DeFranco, Squat University, Cosgrove

FAMILY spanish_squat | Squat: knee dominant isometric
Muscles: Quads (especially patellar tendon loading), glutes
Why: A band behind the knees lets the shins stay vertical while the hips sit back, so the quads work hard with very little compressive stress on the front of the knee. Isometric holds like this are one of the best known tools for calming irritable knees (patellar tendon and kneecap pain) while still loading the quads. A Squat University and physio favourite.
Cues: 1) Heavy band behind both knees anchored to the rig, walk back until it is tight 2) Sit back and down until thighs are around 60 to 90 degrees, shins vertical 3) Keep chest proud, push knees back into the band and hold
Faults: Letting the knees drift forward off the band; Holding the breath for the whole set; Going so deep that pain rises above 3/10
Cautions: Knee friendly by design. Often reduces knee pain for several hours afterwards, which makes it a great warm up before squatting for sore knees. Back is upright so low back load is minimal.
Progress by: Hold longer (45s), sit deeper, add a weight held at the chest, progress to tempo goblet squats and Poliquin step ups
Regress by: Shorten the hold, sit less deep, lighter band, switch to a wall sit with a higher position
Default prescription: 3 to 5 x 20 to 45s holds, rest 60s, about 70% effort (5 x 45s is a classic tendon dose) | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID
Sources: Squat University, Ben Patrick (knee resilience), Rehab research (tendon isometrics)

FAMILY cyclist_squat | Squat: quad biased (heels elevated, narrow)
Muscles: Quads (VMO emphasis), glutes
Why: A narrow stance, heels elevated squat that lets the knees travel well forward. It biases the quads (especially the inner quad) with an upright torso, so it is easy on the low back and great for knee health and quad size. A Poliquin and Squat University favourite.
Cues: 1) Heels on a wedge or plate, feet close together 2) Hold a weight at the chest, stay tall 3) Knees travel forward over the toes, sit straight down, control the bottom
Faults: Bouncing out of the bottom; Heels rolling off the wedge; Leaning the chest forward
Cautions: Very low back friendly. Knees work hard in deep flexion, so build depth gradually for sensitive knees.
Progress by: Add load (DB/KB then SSB), slower tempo (4-1-1-0), pause at the bottom, 1.5 reps
Regress by: Lower heel height, squat to a box, bodyweight with TRX support
Default prescription: 3 sets x 8 to 12 reps, tempo 3-0-1-1, rest 60 to 90s, RPE 7 to 9 | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID
Sources: Poliquin, Squat University, Ben Patrick

FAMILY sumo_squat | Squat: wide stance bilateral
Muscles: Adductors, glutes, quads
Why: A wide stance with toes turned out shifts work towards the inner thighs and glutes and lets people with limited ankle mobility squat deeper with an upright chest. Useful variety and a gentler squat for some knees and hips.
Cues: 1) Feet wide, toes turned out about 30 degrees 2) Knees push out over the toes, sit straight down 3) Squeeze the glutes and inner thighs to stand
Faults: Knees collapsing inward; Going wider than the hips allow; Rounding to reach the weight to the floor
Cautions: Avoid very wide stances with hip pain or groin strains. Good for members whose ankles limit a normal squat.
Progress by: Add load, stand on two boxes for a deficit, pause at the bottom
Regress by: Bodyweight, reduce depth, squat to a box
Default prescription: 3 sets x 8 to 12 reps, tempo 3-0-1-1, rest 60 to 90s, RPE 7 to 9 | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID, BURN
Sources: Cosgrove, Squat University

FAMILY getup | Total body: Turkish get up / windmill
Muscles: Shoulder stability, core, hips, whole body
Why: The get up teaches you to get up from the floor while holding a weight overhead: shoulder stability, hip mobility and confidence in one. Getting off the floor is a key longevity skill (Attia, Dan John, Pavel). Windmills and arm bars build shoulder stability and hip hinge mobility.
Cues: 1) Eyes on the bell, arm locked 2) Move through each step slowly: roll to elbow, hand, high bridge, sweep the leg, kneel, stand 3) Reverse every step with control
Faults: Rushing; Bent arm; Losing sight of the bell
Cautions: Learn with a shoe or no weight first. Shoulder pain: half get up with no load only.
Progress by: Heavier bell, full get up
Regress by: Half get up, shoe on the fist, no weight
Default prescription: 3 to 5 reps each side, slow | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID
Sources: Dan John, Pavel Tsatsouline, Gray Cook

FAMILY pull_up | Vertical pull: pull up / chin up / hangs
Muscles: Lats, upper back, biceps, grip, core
Why: The ultimate upper body bodyweight strength test. Hangs build grip (a strong predictor of longevity) and decompress the spine; negatives and isometrics build strength for the first pull up. LIFTMOR used jumping chin ups with drop landings for bone.
Cues: 1) Grip slightly wider than shoulders (chin up: palms facing you) 2) Pull the shoulder blades down first, then drive the elbows to the ribs 3) Chin over the bar, lower slowly to straight arms
Faults: Kipping or swinging; Half reps; Shrugging at the bottom
Cautions: Shoulder pain: neutral grip, band assistance. Hanging can irritate some shoulders: build up gradually.
Progress by: Band assisted → negatives → bodyweight → weighted
Regress by: Lat pulldown, TRX assisted pull up, scapula pull up, dead hang
Default prescription: 3 to 4 sets x 6 to 10 reps, tempo 3-1-1-0, rest 90 to 120s, RPE 7 to 8 or total reps | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID
Sources: DeFranco, Dan John, Attia (grip), Boyle

FAMILY pulldown | Vertical pull: pulldown / pullover
Muscles: Lats, upper back, biceps
Why: Pulldowns train the pull up pattern at any load, essential for members who can't yet do pull ups. Straight arm pulldowns and pullovers isolate the lats, which also helps overhead mobility.
Cues: 1) Lean back slightly, chest up 2) Pull the shoulder blades down then the elbows to the ribs 3) Return slowly to a full stretch
Faults: Leaning way back and rowing; Pulling behind the neck (shoulder risk); Shortening the range
Cautions: Shoulder friendly with neutral grip. Behind the neck versions: avoid for 40+.
Progress by: Heavier, single arm, tempo, progress to band assisted pull ups
Regress by: Lighter, band pulldown, kneeling
Default prescription: 3 to 4 sets x 6 to 10 reps, tempo 3-1-1-0, rest 90 to 120s, RPE 7 to 8 | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID
Sources: Boyle, Poliquin

FAMILY pike | Vertical push: bodyweight overhead (pike/handstand)
Muscles: Shoulders, triceps, core
Why: Bodyweight overhead pressing progressions. High skill and high shoulder load; good for strong, healthy shoulders only.
Cues: 1) Hips high, hands shoulder width 2) Lower the head between the hands 3) Press back up, keep the core tight
Faults: Flared elbows; Arching the back; Head crashing to the floor
Cautions: Avoid with shoulder pain, high blood pressure (inverted), glaucoma.
Progress by: Elevate the feet, wall handstand holds
Regress by: Pike from knees, DB overhead press
Default prescription: 3 sets x 5 to 10 | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID
Sources: DeFranco

FAMILY overhead_press | Vertical push: overhead press
Muscles: Shoulders, triceps, upper back, core
Why: Pressing overhead builds shoulder strength and the ability to put things on high shelves (a real life task). LIFTMOR used overhead presses for spinal bone density. Requires overhead mobility; half kneeling and single arm versions add core demand and are often easier on the low back.
Cues: 1) Ribs down, glutes tight, wrist stacked over the elbow 2) Press up and slightly back so the biceps finish by the ears 3) Lower with control to the shoulder
Faults: Arching the low back to get the weight up; Pressing forward; Shrugging at the top
Cautions: Needs pain free overhead range (wall test). Shoulder pain: landmine press. Behind the neck pressing: avoid for 40+.
Progress by: Seated → half kneeling → standing → push press; heavier, single arm, bottoms up
Regress by: Landmine press, high incline DB press, lighter weights
Default prescription: 3 to 4 sets x 6 to 10 reps, tempo 3-1-1-0, rest 90 to 120s, RPE 7 to 8 | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID
Sources: Cressey, Poliquin, LIFTMOR research, Dan John

FAMILY dynamic | Warm up: dynamic mobility
Muscles: Whole body
Why: Dynamic drills raise temperature, take joints through range and rehearse patterns (RAMP warm up). The World's Greatest Stretch hits hip flexors, hamstrings, adductors and T spine in one move. 40+ members need a full 8 to 10 minute warm up every session.
Cues: 1) Move through big comfortable ranges 2) Increase range each rep 3) Breathe and stay controlled
Faults: Rushing; Bouncing into range; Skipping the warm up
Cautions: Safe for almost everyone.
Progress by: Bigger range, add reaches
Regress by: Smaller range, hand support
Default prescription: 1 to 2 x 5 to 10 each side (or 20 to 30m) | Sessions: STRONG, HYBRID, BURN
Sources: DeFranco (Limber 11, Simple 6), Gambetta, Squat University

3C. EXERCISES BY CATEGORY

CATEGORY 20. OPTIMAL BRAIN ADDITIONS (COACH RESEARCH) (Signature exercises from the coach research not already in the movement library)
ADD-001 | Patrick Step | G1 | fam poliquin_step | Box / bench / step | S/H | Group Y | S | Up: ATG Split Squat G2; SSB Split Squat G2; Split Squat (DB) G2 | Down: levers | Swap: Poliquin Step Up; Petersen Step Up | About: Ben Patrick's entry level knee builder from ATG Zero: standing on a low step on the ball of the foot, the knee travels forward over the toes as the other heel lowers to tap the floor. It teaches the quads, not the joint, to own the knees over toes position and is the first step towards the ATG split squat.
ADD-002 | ATG Split Squat | G2 | fam split_squat | Bodyweight | S/H | Group Y | S | Up: Barbell Split Squat G3; Band Resisted Split Squat G3; Offset Single Arm Split Squat G3 | Down: Split Squat (Supported) G1; Split Squat (Bodyweight) G1; TRX Assisted Split Squat G1 | Swap: SSB Split Squat; Split Squat (DB); Cable Split Squat | About: Ben Patrick's signature exercise: a long split squat where the front knee travels well past the toes with the heel staying down and the back knee lowers to the floor, giving a deep hip flexor stretch on the back leg. It builds strength in the deepest knee position and hip flexor flexibility at the same time. Start with the front foot elevated and hand support, hold the bottom for time (e.g. 30s), then add reps and load.
ADD-003 | Front Foot Elevated ATG Split Squat (Assisted) | G1 | fam split_squat | Box / bench / step | S/H | Group Y | S | Up: ATG Split Squat G2; SSB Split Squat G2; Split Squat (DB) G2 | Down: levers | Swap: Split Squat (Supported); Split Squat (Bodyweight); TRX Assisted Split Squat | Tags: Assisted | About: The regression Ben Patrick uses before the floor level ATG split squat: front foot on a step and hands on a support so the knee can travel over the toes in a pain free range.
ADD-004 | Reverse Nordic (Assisted) | G2 | fam sissy | Bodyweight | S | Group Y | S | Up: SSB Squat G3; SSB Box Squat G3; Nordic Curl (Full) G3 | Down: Patrick Step G1; Elephant Walk G1; KOT Calf Raise G1 | Swap: Sissy Squat (Assisted); Sissy Squat (Assisted, Short Range) | Watch: Knee | Tags: Assisted | About: Kneeling, the body leans back as one line from knee to head, lengthening and strengthening the quads (especially rectus femoris) through a range most people never train. Ben Patrick uses it for knee resilience and hip flexor length. Use a band or partner for assistance and short range first.
ADD-005 | KOT Calf Raise | G1 | fam calf | Bodyweight | S/H | Group Y | S | Up: ATG Split Squat G2; SSB Split Squat G2; SSB Good Morning G2 | Down: levers | Swap: Single Leg Calf Raise; Calf Raise (Straight Knee); Seated Calf Raise (Bent Knee) | About: Knees over toes calf raise (Ben Patrick): the knee is pushed forward over the toes during the raise, training the calf and Achilles in the bent knee position used in walking downhill, stairs and squatting.
ADD-006 | Tib Bar Tibialis Raise | G1 | fam tib_raise | Tib bar* | S/H/B | Group Y | S | Up: ATG Split Squat G2; SSB Split Squat G2; SSB Good Morning G2 | Down: levers | Swap: Tibialis Raise | About: Loaded tibialis raise using a tib bar or a plate/KB over the feet while seated. Ben Patrick's advanced standard is around 25% bodyweight for 5 x 5. Strong shins protect the knees and ankles and balance the calves.
ADD-007 | Slant Board Goblet Squat | G2 | fam goblet | Kettlebell, Slant board* | S/H/B | Group Y | S | Up: Paused Goblet Squat G3; 1.5 Rep Goblet Squat G3 | Down: Goblet Box Squat G1; Box Squat G1; TRX Assisted Squat G1 | Swap: Goblet Squat (KB); Goblet Squat (DB); Tempo Goblet Squat | About: Squatting with the heels on a slant board lets the knees travel well forward with the torso upright, loading the quads and VMO with less ankle demand. A Ben Patrick and Squat University tool for knee health and quad strength.
ADD-008 | Elephant Walk | G1 | fam stretch | Bodyweight | S/H/B | Group Y | S | Up: ATG Split Squat G2; SSB Split Squat G2; SSB Good Morning G2 | Down: levers | Swap: Frog Stretch; Couch Stretch; Pigeon Stretch | About: ATG hamstring mobility drill: hands on the floor (or blocks), alternate bending and straightening each knee to walk the hamstrings into length. Loaded or unloaded, used in Ben Patrick's ATG Zero.
ADD-009 | SSB Squat | G3 | fam ssb_squat | Safety squat bar | S | Group Y | Up: SSB Pause Squat G4; Anderson Squat G4; Tempo Back Squat G4 | Down: Barbell Box Squat G2; Banded Barbell Box Squat G2 | Swap: SSB Box Squat; Zercher Squat; DB Front Squat | Tags: Safety squat bar
ADD-010 | SSB Box Squat | G3 | fam ssb_squat | Safety squat bar, Box / bench / step | S | Group Y | Up: SSB Pause Squat G4; Anderson Squat G4; Tempo Back Squat G4 | Down: Barbell Box Squat G2; Banded Barbell Box Squat G2 | Swap: SSB Squat; Zercher Squat; DB Front Squat | Tags: Safety squat bar
ADD-011 | SSB Pause Squat | G4 | fam ssb_squat | Safety squat bar | S | Group Y | Up: levers | Down: SSB Squat G3; SSB Box Squat G3 | Swap: Anderson Squat; Tempo Back Squat; Paused Back Squat | Tags: Paused, Safety squat bar
ADD-012 | SSB Split Squat | G2 | fam split_squat | Safety squat bar | S/H | Group Y | S | Up: Barbell Split Squat G3; Band Resisted Split Squat G3; Offset Single Arm Split Squat G3 | Down: Split Squat (Supported) G1; Split Squat (Bodyweight) G1; TRX Assisted Split Squat G1 | Swap: ATG Split Squat; Split Squat (DB); Cable Split Squat | Tags: Safety squat bar | About: Split squat with the safety squat bar: allows heavier single leg loading than dumbbells without grip limiting, and keeps the shoulders comfortable.
ADD-013 | SSB Reverse Lunge | G2 | fam reverse_lunge | Safety squat bar | S/H/B | Group Y | S | Up: Slider Reverse Lunge G3; Deficit Reverse Lunge G3; Barbell Reverse Lunge G3 | Down: Split Squat (Supported) G1; Split Squat (Bodyweight) G1; TRX Assisted Split Squat G1 | Swap: Reverse Lunge (DB); Cable Reverse Lunge; Goblet Reverse Lunge | Tags: Safety squat bar | About: Reverse lunge loaded with the safety squat bar for heavy single leg strength without grip or shoulder limitations.
ADD-014 | SSB Good Morning | G2 | fam good_morning | Safety squat bar, Barbell | S/H | Group Y | Up: Barbell Good Morning G3; Barbell Romanian Deadlift G3; Paused DB RDL G3 | Down: Bodyweight Good Morning G1; Hip Hinge to Wall G1; Hip Hinge Dowel Drill G1 | Swap: Seated Good Morning; Banded Good Morning; Single Leg Good Morning | Watch: Low back | Tags: Safety squat bar | About: Good morning with the safety squat bar: the handles and padding make it more comfortable and safer to learn than a straight bar good morning. Builds hamstrings and back extensors. Teams G3 to G4 only and not for low back pain.
ADD-015 | Lunge Matrix (Forward, Lateral, Rotational, Reverse) | G1 | fam dynamic | Bodyweight | S/H/B | Group Y | S | Up: ATG Split Squat G2; SSB Split Squat G2; SSB Good Morning G2 | Down: levers | Swap: A March; Inchworm; Crab Reach | About: Vern Gambetta's multi directional lunge sequence: a lunge forward, to the side, rotational (turning 135 degrees) and backward on each leg. It warms up the hips in all three planes and builds balance and multi plane strength. Ideal warm up for golf and 40+ hips.
ADD-016 | Limber 11 (DeFranco Lower Body Routine) | G1 | fam dynamic | Bodyweight | S/H/B | Group Y | S | Up: ATG Split Squat G2; SSB Split Squat G2; SSB Good Morning G2 | Down: levers | Swap: A March; Inchworm; Crab Reach | About: Joe DeFranco's 10 to 15 minute lower body mobility routine: foam roll IT band and adductors, lacrosse ball glutes, bent knee iron cross, roll overs into V sits, rocking frog stretch, fire hydrant circles, slow mountain climbers, Cossack squats, seated piriformis stretch, rear foot elevated hip flexor stretch. Optimal uses a shortened 5 to 6 drill version before lower body sessions.
ADD-017 | Simple 6 (DeFranco Upper Body Routine) | G1 | fam dynamic | Bodyweight | S/H/B | Group Y | S | Up: ATG Split Squat G2; SSB Split Squat G2; SSB Good Morning G2 | Down: levers | Swap: A March; Inchworm; Crab Reach | About: Joe DeFranco's quick upper body mobility routine for shoulders and thoracic spine (band pass throughs, wall slides, scapular and T spine drills, lat and pec stretches). Use before pressing and pulling days.
ADD-018 | McGill Big 3 (Curl Up, Side Plank, Bird Dog) | G1 | fam mcgill_curl | Bodyweight | S/H | Group Y | S | Up: ATG Split Squat G2; SSB Split Squat G2; SSB Good Morning G2 | Down: levers | Swap: McGill Curl Up | About: Professor Stuart McGill's daily core routine for back health: modified curl up, side plank and bird dog, each held 8 to 10 seconds in a descending pyramid (e.g. 6, 4, 2 reps). Builds core endurance and stiffness without stressing the discs. Default warm up for any member with back history.
ADD-019 | 4 Point TVA Draw In (Chek) | G1 | fam breathing | Bodyweight | S/H/B | Group Y | S | Up: ATG Split Squat G2; SSB Split Squat G2; SSB Good Morning G2 | Down: levers | Swap: Pelvic Tilt; 90/90 Breathing; Pelvic Tilt (Supine) | About: Paul Chek's inner unit drill: on hands and knees, let the belly relax then draw the belly button gently towards the spine without moving the spine, hold and breathe. Teaches deep core activation before loading (stabilisers before prime movers).
ADD-020 | Prone Cobra (Chek) | G2 | fam back_ext | Bodyweight | S/H | Group Y | S | Up: SSB Squat G3; SSB Box Squat G3; Nordic Curl (Full) G3 | Down: Reverse Hyper (Bench, Light) G1; 45 Degree Back Extension (Bodyweight) G1 | Swap: Reverse Hyper (Bench); Superman (Alternating); 45 Degree Back Extension | About: Paul Chek's postural endurance exercise: lying face down, lift the chest slightly and rotate the thumbs out to squeeze the shoulder blades, hold. Strengthens the upper back and spinal extensors that hold upright posture.
ADD-021 | Swiss Ball Supine Lateral Ball Roll (Chek) | G2 | fam rotation | Swiss ball* | H/S | Group Y | S | Up: SSB Squat G3; SSB Box Squat G3; Nordic Curl (Full) G3 | Down: Patrick Step G1; Elephant Walk G1; KOT Calf Raise G1 | Swap: Landmine 180; Landmine Rainbow; Landmine Rotation | Tags: Swiss | About: Paul Chek's twist pattern exercise: lying with the shoulders on a Swiss ball and hips up, arms out holding a light weight, shuffle sideways across the ball keeping the body level. Trains rotational stability of the trunk and hips.
ADD-022 | Floor to Stand Transfer | G2 | fam getup | Bodyweight | S/H | Group Y | Up: SSB Squat G3; SSB Box Squat G3; Nordic Curl (Full) G3 | Down: Half Turkish Get Up G1; Turkish Get Up (Light) G1 | Swap: KB Windmill; Arm Bar (KB); Turkish Get Up | Watch: Shoulder | About: Getting down to the floor and back up with as little support as possible. A key longevity skill (Attia, Dan John) and part of the Optimal screen. Progress from using a chair to hands only to no hands.
ADD-023 | Half Kneeling to Stand | G2 | fam getup | Bodyweight | S/H | Group Y | Up: SSB Squat G3; SSB Box Squat G3; Nordic Curl (Full) G3 | Down: Half Turkish Get Up G1; Turkish Get Up (Light) G1 | Swap: KB Windmill; Arm Bar (KB); Turkish Get Up | Watch: Shoulder | Tags: Kneeling | About: From half kneeling, stand up without using the hands (then lower back down). Builds the strength and balance needed to get off the floor, and is a stepping stone to the Turkish get up.
ADD-024 | Fast Sit to Stand (Power) | G1 | fam sit_to_stand | Box / bench / step | S/H | Group Y | S | Up: ATG Split Squat G2; SSB Split Squat G2; SSB Good Morning G2 | Down: levers | Swap: Sit to Stand (Chair); Squat to Box Stand Up (Bodyweight) | Tags: Fast/power | About: Stand up from a chair as fast as possible, sit down slowly. Trains power, which declines faster than strength with age, using the safest possible movement. Seniors programme staple.
ADD-025 | Tandem Stance Balance | G1 | fam balance | Bodyweight | S/H | Group Y | S | Up: ATG Split Squat G2; SSB Split Squat G2; SSB Good Morning G2 | Down: levers | Swap: Heel to Toe Walk; Single Leg Balance; Star Excursion Balance | About: Standing heel to toe (one foot directly in front of the other) near a support. A falls prevention staple and seniors programme warm up.
ADD-026 | Heel to Toe Walk | G1 | fam balance | Bodyweight | S/H | Group Y | S | Up: ATG Split Squat G2; SSB Split Squat G2; SSB Good Morning G2 | Down: levers | Swap: Single Leg Balance; Tandem Stance Balance; Star Excursion Balance | About: Walking in a straight line placing the heel directly in front of the toes. Trains dynamic balance and gait (Gambetta: postural alignment and dynamic balance are the foundation).
ADD-027 | Clock Reach (Single Leg Balance) | G1 | fam balance | Bodyweight | S/H | Group Y | S | Up: ATG Split Squat G2; SSB Split Squat G2; SSB Good Morning G2 | Down: levers | Swap: Heel to Toe Walk; Single Leg Balance; Tandem Stance Balance | About: Standing on one leg and reaching the other foot to points on an imaginary clock. Builds balance, ankle and hip control.
ADD-028 | Jumping Chin Up with Drop Landing (LIFTMOR) | G3 | fam pull_up | Rig / pull up bar | S/H | Group N | impact high | T | Up: Weighted Pull Up G4; Weighted Chin Up G4; Commando Pull Up G4 | Down: Hanging (Supported) G2; TRX Assisted Pull Up G2; Band Assisted Pull Up G2 | Swap: Pull Up; Chin Up; Dead Hang | Watch: Pelvic floor, Shoulder | About: Used in the LIFTMOR trial for postmenopausal bone health: jump to the top of a chin up, lower slowly, then drop and land firmly on both feet. The landing impact loads the hips and spine. Supervised Teams only, screened, no pelvic floor symptoms.
ADD-029 | Heel Drops (Bone Loading) | G1 | fam calf | Bodyweight | S/H | Group Y | impact low | S | Up: ATG Split Squat G2; SSB Split Squat G2; SSB Good Morning G2 | Down: levers | Swap: KOT Calf Raise; Single Leg Calf Raise; Calf Raise (Straight Knee) | About: Rise onto the toes and drop firmly onto the heels. A low level impact progression used to load bone in members not yet ready for jumps. No flight phase, so it can be used in groups if comfortable.
ADD-030 | Step Back Burpee (No Jump) | G3 | fam burpee | Bodyweight | B/H | Group Y | Up: SSB Pause Squat G4 | Down: ATG Split Squat G2; SSB Split Squat G2; SSB Good Morning G2 | Swap: Squat Thrust to Bench (No Jump); Sprawls; Burpees | Watch: Low back, Osteoporosis/osteopenia, Pelvic floor | About: The Optimal group burpee: hands down, step back to plank, step in, stand tall. All of the metabolic benefit with no jumping or landing.
ADD-031 | Squat Thrust to Bench (No Jump) | G3 | fam burpee | Box / bench / step | B/H | Group Y | Up: SSB Pause Squat G4 | Down: ATG Split Squat G2; SSB Split Squat G2; SSB Good Morning G2 | Swap: Step Back Burpee (No Jump); Sprawls; Burpees | Watch: Low back, Osteoporosis/osteopenia, Pelvic floor | About: Hands on a bench, step back and in. A no impact, wrist friendly burpee regression for groups and seniors.
ADD-032 | Step Jacks (No Jump) | G2 | fam jacks | Bodyweight | B | Group Y | Up: SSB Squat G3; SSB Box Squat G3; Nordic Curl (Full) G3 | Down: Patrick Step G1; Elephant Walk G1; KOT Calf Raise G1 | Swap: Skipping; Star Jumps; Jumping Jacks | Watch: Knee, Osteoporosis/osteopenia, Pelvic floor | About: Step one foot out to the side as the arms rise overhead, alternate sides. Low impact jumping jack alternative for groups.
ADD-033 | Marching High Knees | G1 | fam dynamic | Bodyweight | S/H/B | Group Y | S | Up: ATG Split Squat G2; SSB Split Squat G2; SSB Good Morning G2 | Down: levers | Swap: A March; Inchworm; Crab Reach | About: Driving the knees up in a fast march with no hop. A no impact swap for high knees in group warm ups and finishers.
ADD-034 | Zone 2 Steady State (Bike, Rower or Ski) | G1 | fam erg | Assault bike / bike, Rower, Ski erg | B/H | Group Y | S | Up: ATG Split Squat G2; SSB Split Squat G2; SSB Good Morning G2 | Down: levers | Swap: Rower and Ski Ladder; Sprint Intervals 10 to 20s (Bike or Ski); Rower 500m | About: 20 to 45 minutes at a pace where you can just hold a conversation (RPE 3 to 4). Builds the aerobic base, mitochondrial health and fat burning capacity; Peter Attia's longevity foundation. Usually done outside group sessions (walks, cycling) or as recovery.
ADD-035 | VO2 Max Intervals (4 x 4 min) | G1 | fam erg | Bodyweight | B/H | Group Y | S | Up: ATG Split Squat G2; SSB Split Squat G2; SSB Good Morning G2 | Down: levers | Swap: Rower 500m; Tabata Bike; Bike (Easy) | About: 4 to 5 intervals of 3 to 4 minutes hard (RPE 8 to 9) with equal easy recovery on the bike, rower or ski. Improves peak aerobic capacity, one of the strongest predictors of longevity (Attia). Teams, once per week maximum.
ADD-036 | Sprint Intervals 10 to 20s (Bike or Ski) | G1 | fam erg | Assault bike / bike, Ski erg | B/H | Group Y | S | Up: ATG Split Squat G2; SSB Split Squat G2; SSB Good Morning G2 | Down: levers | Swap: Zone 2 Steady State (Bike, Rower or Ski); Tabata Bike; Bike (Easy) | Tags: Fast/power | About: 6 to 10 all out efforts of 10 to 20 seconds with 1 to 2 minutes easy. Stacy Sims recommends this short, sharp style for peri and post menopausal women; Cosgrove ranks intervals above steady cardio for fat loss. Zero impact on the bike or ski erg.
ADD-037 | Trap Bar Deadlift (Low Handles) | G3 | fam trap_bar | Trap bar* | S | Group Y | Up: Rack Pull G4; Block Pull G4; Sumo Deadlift G4 | Down: Trap Bar RDL G2; Trap Bar Deadlift (High Handles) G2; Tempo DB RDL G2 | Swap: Trap Bar Deadlift; Paused DB RDL; Barbell Romanian Deadlift
ADD-038 | Nordic Curl (Full) | G3 | fam ham_curl | Bodyweight | S/H | Group Y | Up: SSB Pause Squat G4; Single Leg Slider Curl G4 | Down: TRX Hamstring Curl G2; Swiss Ball Hamstring Curl G2; Hamstring Curl (Swiss Ball) G2 | Swap: Slider Hamstring Curl; Hamstring Curl (Slider); Single Leg Swiss Ball Curl
RHP-030 Banded Hip Distraction: see Rehab: Hip

3D. VARIATION TAGS
Combination move: Combination move: trains two qualities at once, efficient for HYBRID and BURN; keep technique of both parts clean before speeding up.
Assisted: Assisted version: support lets members practise the full pattern safely while strength builds (Cosgrove: regress to progress).
Landmine: Landmine: the arc path is shoulder and back friendly and very easy to learn.
Isometric: Isometric: builds strength and tendon tolerance in a fixed position with minimal joint irritation.
Swiss: Swiss ball adds instability for core control (Paul Chek); keep loads modest.
Barbell: Barbell: allows the heaviest loading for maximum strength and bone stimulus; earn it with technique (usually G3 to G4, Teams).
Paused: Paused version: holding the hardest position removes the bounce and builds strength exactly where people usually fail.
Heels elevated: Heels elevated: compensates for stiff ankles, keeps the torso upright and biases the quads (Squat University).
Tempo: Tempo version: a slow 3 to 5 second lowering builds control, tendon strength and muscle at lighter loads (Poliquin), ideal for joint sensitive members.
1.5 rep: 1.5 rep version: an extra half rep in the bottom range doubles time in the hardest position (Poliquin) without needing heavier weights.
Pulses keep constant tension in the bott: Pulses keep constant tension in the bottom range: metabolic and BURN friendly.
Bottoms: Bottoms up kettlebell forces the grip and rotator cuff to stabilise; a brilliant (light) shoulder rehab tool.
The: The mini band cues the knees out and switches on the glute med.
Band: Band resistance gets harder at the top (accommodating resistance) and can cue better positions such as knees out.
Cable: Cable: constant tension through the whole range with smooth, joint friendly resistance.
One: One sided loading adds an anti rotation / anti lateral flexion core challenge and exposes left/right differences.
Wide: Wide grip/stance shifts emphasis (chest/upper back or adductors) and can be less friendly for some shoulders and hips.
Double KB: Double KB: heavier total load plus a front rack or bilateral grip demand.
Sliders: Sliders add a friction based eccentric control challenge.
Knee: Knee drive adds hip flexor strength and single leg balance at the top.
Reduced range/load: Reduced range/load version: used for rehab, beginners, warm ups or flare up days.
Deficit: Deficit version: increases range of motion for more strength and mobility in the bottom position.
Weighted: Weighted: progress to this once 12 to 15 bodyweight reps are easy.
Split: Split stance adds a stability and anti rotation demand closer to real life movement.
Close: Close grip shifts work towards the triceps.
Kneeling: Kneeling position locks the hips so the core has to control the trunk and stops the legs cheating.
Neutral: Neutral grip (palms facing) is the most shoulder and elbow friendly grip.
Feet elevated: Feet elevated: more bodyweight on the arms for a harder variation.
Chest supported: Chest supported: removes the low back from the lift so the upper back can be trained hard (ideal for back pain).
Fast/power: Fast/power version: move with intent, stop when speed drops.
Safety squat bar: Safety squat bar: heavy loading without needing shoulder mobility to hold a straight bar; DeFranco's go to for cranky shoulders.
